D | news about whole company arrive:pr before word about person whom you love:pr. joy for other:pl with private:m fear together live:pr. | That interval when the boat has been sighted and the town cheers, but you still cannot pick out the face you went down to see.

## Give the hill your other eye | hill:d your/other eye give:imp | idiom-622
D | tired:m watch:ag relieve:inf so that they away look:can:pr and still hope keep:pr. also of night near sick:m person. | Take over a watch in words that let the other person look away. It carries an assurance that they may sleep and still be the first one you wake.

## To patch the dry sleeve | dry:m sleeve patch_repair:inf | idiom-623

## No bell for a maybe | for perhaps no bell | idiom-626
D | report uncertain:m through public:m voice send:neg:imp. first who see:pa ask:imp, especially when kin wait:pr. | A request to find the first witness before giving uncertain news a public voice. It applies as much to a marvelous catch as to word of a missing boat.

## To quarrel with a dry pillow | with dry:m pillow quarrel:inf | idiom-627
D | angry:m be:inf with absent:m beloved for danger which they choose:pa, when you also their/return in safety desire:pr. anger and love same:m bed share:pr. | To be furious with someone for sailing and frightened for them in the very same bed. ## The wind owes two answers | wind two answer:pl owe:pr | idiom-629

## To outwait the gulls | gull_seabird:pl than long:m wait:inf | idiom-630

# Care at arm’s length and nearer | care at arm:g length and near | section-8

## Ask which end of the blanket | which end of coverlet ask:imp | idiom-98

## The wind under somebody else's cloth | wind under other:m person:g cloth | idiom-675

E | for kjv_inn+keeper market_fair_assembly three day:pl of silver be:pa. for i three night:pl of wash:inf. wind under other:m person:g cloth. | The fair meant three days of silver for the innkeeper and three nights of washing for me. Wind under somebody else's cloth.

## The third hand on the helm | third:m hand on helm_steering | idiom-676
D | help which no person ask:pa. it common:m movement of two who already together work:pr spoil:pr. give:ag see:pr need for effort and miss:pr need for one_num direction. | An extra helper whose intervention spoils a movement two people already have in hand. It is the competing direction that causes the mischief.
E | wepl door fit:pprog when uncle his/shoulder add:pa. third:m hand on helm_steering, brother say:pa, and door upon floor lie:pa. | We were fitting the door when Uncle added his shoulder. “Third hand on the helm,” my brother said from behind the fallen door.

## A patch that knows the wind | patch which wind know:pr | idiom-677
D | repair which from sign:pl of wear learn:pr where strength need:psv:pr. mark of first:m fail:ev in mend:psv:pp work remain:pr. | A repair that answers the particular way a thing has failed. You can read the history of the strain in the shape of the patch.
E | child always by left:m corner pocket tear:pa. mother patch which wind know:pr make:pa and above that corner cloth double:pa. | My child always tore the left corner of her pocket. Mother made a patch that knew the wind and doubled the cloth above that corner.

## To lend the boat your knees | boat:d thy/knee:pl lend:inf | idiom-678
D | change to which thou thyself yield:must:pr that what wepl together carry:pr in balance remain:pr. helper who bend:neg:pr each movement greater:m make:pr. | To absorb a little movement yourself so the thing everyone is carrying stays steady. The person who stiffens against every change passes each jolt on.
E | when with child this song sing:pr thou, boat:d thy/knee:pl lend:imp. where she breath take:pr one_num beat wait:imp. | Lend the boat your knees when you sing this with the child. Wait a beat where she takes her breath.

## Give the rope a floor | binding_rope:d floor give:imp | idiom-680
D | before swift:m work begin:pr, room make:imp for thing:pl which in succession move:must:pr. disorder at beginning speed in hour of need steal:pr. | Make room beforehand for things that must run in order. The coil laid clear on deck gives its owner time just when time becomes scarce.

## To change seats with the ballast | with damat_ballast place:pl change:inf | idiom-682
D | from place of command into humble:m need:psv:pp work go:inf when that work ship in balance keep:pr. person own rank carry:neg:pr into new:m place. | To exchange a conspicuous office for the humble job that holds the enterprise steady just now. Said with affection when the former captain takes orders there.
E | our/judge with damat_ballast place:pl change:pa. while nurse child bind:pa he as she command:pa water carry:pa. | Our judge changed seats with the ballast. While the nurse bandaged the child he carried water exactly as she told him.

## The sail speaks through the rope | sail through binding_rope speak:pr | idiom-683
D | distant:m change in thy/hand feel:inf because work thou do:pr to it bind:psv:pr. from unseen:m cause change straight:m to that hand come:pr. | A change far away reaches someone through the ordinary material of their work. The hand holding the line can give evidence about a sail it cannot see.

## One boot on each tack | one_num boot on each damat_tack | idiom-684
D | to two contrary:m undertaking:pl self promise:inf and hope movement of each thy/other promise hide:fu. | To promise your presence to two undertakings that move in opposite directions, in the hope that movement at each will cover your absence from the other.

## Send the bucket up empty | kjv_bucket empty:m up send:imp | idiom-685
D | give:imp learn:ag first turn with little:m weight so they whole movement before burden learn:can:pr. even empty:m kjv_bucket its/own way have:pr. | Let a beginner discover the whole movement before giving it weight. An empty bucket still swings and still has to clear the rail.
E | apprentice:d first day no money give:pa aunt. empty:m kjv_bucket up send:imp. without coin_currency market:g way and merchant:g name learn:imp. | Aunt gave the apprentice no money on his first day. “Send the bucket up empty. Learn the market road and the merchant's name without a coin.”

## A sailful of yesterday | sail full:m of yesterday_day | idiom-687
D | forward:m movement after cause which make:pa it possible:m already pass:perf. person still move:pr and suppose:pr aid still arrive:pr. | The last little run of a boat after its wind has fallen. Said of a success still spending help that has already ceased.
E | market_stall for one_num month after mother die:pa prosper:pa. order:pl which she to merchant:pl give:pa still arrive:pa. sail full:m of yesterday_day carry:pa wepl:a. | The shop prospered for a month after Mother died. The orders she had placed were still coming in. A sailful of yesterday carried us.

# Work in company | labour with companion:pl | section-14

It becomes a rebuke when the singer begins scolding a quiet companion. An exchange of ends usually settles the question.
E | why sing:neg:pr you? he ask:pa. his/sister long:m end of chest to he give:pa. now short:m end sing:pr, say:pa she. | “Why aren’t you singing?” His sister gave him her end of the chest. “Now the short end sings.”

## A loaf at each elbow | kjv_loaf at each elbow | idiom-208
D | give:inf to labour+person:pl while at work be:pr they without require:cv they leave:inf their/place or ask:inf favour. especially for labour+person:pl who place or sick:m person or unfinished:m work leave:must:pr if they go:pr to fetch:inf meal. | Practical hospitality that reaches workers where they stand and spares them a petition. ## To inherit the wet sleeve | wet:m sleeve:a inherit:inf | idiom-210

## A name on both shifts | name on both watch:pl | idiom-211
D | separate:m group:pl each reckon:pr same:m helper as in full for self, so whole strength seem:pr greater:m than it be:pr. both promise:pl sincere:m be:can:pr. false:m reckoning plain:m become:pr when both group:pl at same:m hour person:a need:pr. | A worker two crews have each counted as fully their own. ## The needle has two owners | needle two owner:pl have:pr | idiom-670

## The rope’s quiet guest | binding_rope:g quiet:m guest | idiom-212

## The empty nail stays up | empty:m nail remain:pr | idiom-215

# At the market | at market | section-15

## A price with a doorstep | price with threshold | idiom-224
D | price low:m until cost of reach:inf place or carry:inf thing home reckon:psv:pr. | An attractive price that stops at the seller’s door. The buyer still has to get there and bring the goods home.
E | at other:m bank flour_food cheap:m be:pr. price with threshold be:pr that. wepl boat there and back pay:must:pr. | “Flour is cheaper across the river.” “That price has a doorstep. We’d pay for the boat both ways.”

## The far bank sets the price | far:m bank price set:pr | idiom-707
D | bargain which cheap:m appear:pr before cost of carry:ev home know:psv:pr. ask:imp those who other shore reach:perf. | A price that becomes knowable only on the other side. Island buyers say it of bargains whose real cost is the crossing, the handling, or what spoils on the way.
E | pear:pl almost free:m be:pa. far:m bank price set:pa. half in broken:m kjv_basket soft:m become:pa. | The pears were almost free. The far bank set the price. Half went soft in a broken basket.

## Twelve to the ear | twelve to ear | idiom-225

A courteous buyer can call for a recount this way while leaving the seller room to have made an honest mistake.

## Some silver still smells of hay | some silver still of hay smell:pr | idiom-237
D | money for known:m work receive:psv:pp which owner in reckon:cv still that work remember:pr. spend:inf kjv_difficult:m be:can:pr. price in day:pl of work in secret reckon:pr its/owner. milton_hesitation exact:m memory of labour show:can:pr. | Money whose particular labor you still feel when you take it out to spend. E | three full:m wagon:pl for that little:m mirror, say:pa girl. her/silver still of hay smell:pa. mirror leave:pa she. | “Three full wagons for that little mirror.” Her silver still smelled of hay. She left the mirror there.

## The door in the floor | door in floor | idiom-335

E | my/cup fall:pa. door in floor open:pa and i upon snow among sheep stand:pa. | My cup fell. The door in the floor opened, and I was standing in the snow among sheep.

## A feather on the tongue | feather on tongue | idiom-336

D | after long:m silence first:m word:pl old:m dust wash:pr away. before collect:inf what person mean:pr, let:imp these word:pl run:inf. | A kindness between people meeting after a long silence. The first rush of words clears the roof of old dust. What they really have to say will come afterward.
E | first:m rain to roof belong:pr, mother say:pa. about weather for hour she:d i speak:pa before about lost:m child ask:pa i. | “The first rain belongs to the roof,” Mother said. We talked about the weather for an hour before I asked about the child she had lost.

## A hinge on the cistern | kjv_hinge on cistern_reservoir | idiom-815

## Salt at the bottom of the well | salt at bottom of kjv_well | idiom-816

## The roof that drinks for two | roof which for two drink:pr | idiom-817
D | share of good:m fortune which come:pr from how house build:psv:perf before luck arrive:pa. neighbour same:m rain receive:pr but less water store:pr. | An advantage that comes from arrangements laid down before luck arrived. Two houses have the same rain, and one roof drinks for two.

## Two mouths to the coconut | two mouth:pl to damat_coconut | idiom-819

## Breadfruit at the back door | dampac_breadfruit at back door | idiom-821

## A little island inside the island | little:m island inside island | idiom-825

## The calabash keeps your thumb | dam_calabash your/thumb_digit keep:pr | idiom-826

## Let the island grow smaller at supper | at supper island less:m become:imp | idiom-827
D | while food share:psv:pr, long:m distance of day quarrel shorten:imp. when path to table again short:m be:pr, quarrel later settle:can:psv:pr. | A host’s invitation to shorten for one meal the distances a quarrel has made. E | forty year:pl net:pl make:perf i and still when knot slip:pr my/uncle:g wood_splinter of ease feel:pr i. | I have made nets for forty years and still feel my uncle’s splinter of easy when a mesh slips.

## The other hand of knowing | other:m hand of know:ev | idiom-526
D | person right:m thing when show:psv:pr know:pr though alone it make:neg:can:pr. this knowledge own:m boundary have:pr. | Recognizing the right thing when someone shows it to you, though you could not produce it alone. It is a real kind of knowledge with a particular limit.

## To lose a good mistake | good:m error lose:inf | idiom-527

## A cup of yesterday’s courage | cup of yesterday_day:g courage | idiom-529

## The hole shaped like the answer | hole in form of answer | idiom-532

# The timing of laughter | time of laugh:ev | section-33

## A laugh with its shoes off | laugh:ev with shoe:pl remove:psv:perf | idiom-538

## To save the wrong laugh | wrong:m laugh:ev save:inf | idiom-541

# Marks, measures and the making of pictures | mark:pl measure:pl and picture make:ev | section-35
